J.League Division 2

Promotion to J.League Division 1: 

Top 2 teams will be promoted automatically. The 3rd promoting club will be 
determined by a play-off held by 4 clubs (3rd - 6th placed clubs in the standings 
in the league matches). 

First Round: Two ties will be held: "3rd placed club - 6th placed club" and 
"4th placed club - 5th placed club". Each tie will be played as one match on 
the home stadium determined by the standings in the league matches 
(i.e. 3rd and 4th placed clubs' stadiums).

Final Round: A tie will be held by the two winners of the first round. The tie 
will be played as one match at a neutral venue, which is yet to be determined.

Tiebreakers: If the score ties by 90 minutes, neither extra time nor penalty shoot-out 
will be held: the winner is determined by the standings in the league matches.

JFL

Promotion to J.League Division 2: 

For promotion the club must hold J.League Associate Membership and 
pass a final inspection by J.League [#]. 
1st placed JFL club will promote to J2 automatically if it meets promotion criteria.
2nd placed JFL club will promote to J2 if it meets promotion criteria and wins a 
home-and-away play-off against a J2 club.

       Y.S.C.C. = Yokohama Sports and Culture Club.
       
       On 16 October 2012 it was reported that Sagawa Shiga FC 
       had notified JFL organization about their intention of 
       withdrawal from JFL as of the end of the season. 
       Because of withdrawals by Arte Takasaki and Sagawa Shiga, 
       no clubs are to be relegated automatically. The bottom finished 
       (17th placed) club will play a play-off against the 3rd placed club 
       in 2012 All Japan Regional Football Promotion League Series.

Regional Leagues

In Japan, regional leagues are made of 9 regional championship. 
Champions and some runners-up qualify for national regional league 
tournament wich decide the entry into JFL.9

Hokkaido | Tohoku | Kanto | Hoku-shin-etsu | Tokai | Kansai | Chugoku | Shikoku | Kyushu

48th All-Syakaijin Cup


Introduction: 

The shakaijin tournament is a national tournament for Japan cat. 1 –
shakaijin (adults) teams only.

1 – 47 Prefectoral tournaments for shakaijin teams from prefectoral 
    leagues only, winners enter the regional tournaments with teams 
    from regional leagues.

2 - 9 Regional tournaments for the winners of the prefectoral cups and 
    teams from regional leagues. Winners qualifies for the National 
    tournament

3 – The National shakaijin tournament held in the prefecture of Tokyo

Both finalists (or semi-finalists) would be eligible to enter the 36th 
National Regional leagues promotion tournament which decides the entry 
into the JFL, nationwide 3rd division.

The following results start at the regional stage, schedules and 
tournament format could be different from a region to another.

Note: Top 3 teams qualify for the 36th National Regional Leagues Tournament, 
      which decides promotion to JFL. As Fukushima United FC had already 
      qualified as the winner of Tohoku Regional League, their place were 
      given to Banditonce Kakogawa.

JFL promotion play-off

JFL Promotion Tournament 

Participating teams: 
Regional Leagues winners (9 teams); 
Top teams of 48th Shakaijin National Tournament (3 teams). 

Tournament format: 
First round:
3 groups of 4 teams each are playing each other once.
The top teams and the best runner-up qualify for the Final round. 

Final round and promotion into JFL:
4 teams playing each other once in a single group.
Top 2 teams are promoted to JFL directly. The 3rd placed team 
Participates in a play-off against the bottom-placed team of JFL. 

NB: On every stage drawn matches are decided by penalty shoot-outs, 
    the winners obtaining 2 pts, the losers – 1 pt.
